This provides salt bridge potential, contributing to stability, but the amount is not extreme.<br>- **Proline:** Present at moderate levels (PPDL, TGR, etc.) — not excessively high as seen in hyperthermophiles.<br>- **Glycine:** Present in significant amounts throughout, suggesting some flexibility — not as few as in thermophiles, nor as many as in psychrophiles.<br>- **Thermolabile residues (N, Q, C):** Present in notable quantities (NGL, NV, NI, NY, NS, CIG, etc.) — these would be depleted in hyperthermophilic proteins.<br>- **Hydrophobic residues:** Well-distributed, providing a stable core.<br><br>**Evaluating Each Option:**<br><br>- **A (Cold-adapted):** Unlikely. Thermophilic proteins typically show higher charged residue fractions, fewer thermolabile residues, and more proline than what we observe here.<br>- **D (Extremely thermostable at 90°C):** Unlikely. <td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which condition is associated with increased expression of this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> It is up-regulated in response to heat stress.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> It is up-regulated in response to nutrient deprivation.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> It is up-regulated in response to DNA damage.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> It is up-regulated in response to mitochondrial stress.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Protein Identification:**<br>Analyzing the protein sequence (~860 amino acids), I can identify key domain features:<br>- **Two kinase domains**: The first kinase domain contains a &quot;GNPVIP&quot; linker motif between the two kinase domains (a hallmark of PKR-like kinases), a &quot;DWSGI&quot; DFG-like motif. The second kinase domain has the classic &quot;GEGTYG&quot; glycine-rich loop and &quot;ITHAHQ&quot; catalytic residues.<br>- **A PPIase domain** near the C-terminus (identified by &quot;GDTKPCD...GKD&quot; motifs characteristic of FKBP-type peptidyl-prolyl isomerase).<br>- **N-terminal region** with hydrophobic elements that could serve as a targeting signal.<br><br>The **two kinase domains separated by a &quot;GNP&quot; linker** is the defining architectural feature of the **PKR (Protein Kinase R-like Endoplasmic Reticulum Kinase, EIF2AK family)** family of kinases. The GNP motif specifically points to PKR-like architecture.<br><br>**Regulatory Context:**<br>PKR (EIF2AK2) is a stress-sensing kinase that:<br>- Is activated by double-stranded RNA (dsRNA)<br>- Can be triggered by **mitochondrial dsRNA** released during mitochondrial dysfunction/stress<br>- Connects to the **Integrated Stress Response (ISR)** pathway through eIF2α phosphorylation<br>- Is upregulated during mitochondrial stress via type I IFN signaling triggered by mitochondrial nucleic acid release (through MAVS/cGAS-STING pathways)<br>- Has been demonstrated to increase in expression and activation in multiple mitochondrial disease models<br><br>**Evaluating alternatives:**<br>- A (Heat stress): No specific connection between PKR-like kinases and heat shock induction<br>- B (Nutrient deprivation): More closely associated with GCN2, which has a different domain architecture (histidyl-tRNA binding domain)<br>- C (DNA damage): Checkpoint kinases (Chk1, Chk2) have single kinase domains; ATM/ATR are much larger<br>- D (Mitochondrial stress): Consistent with PKR-like kinase architecture and known regulation<br><br>2. answer: D</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> A sequence-based guess about a protein’s broad molecular function is not enough to infer its specific expression response. Which condition is associated with increased expression of this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Heat stress<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Nutrient deprivation<br><br><strong>C.</strong> DNA damage<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Mitochondrial stress<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> GO_biological_process_0101:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>Analyzing the protein sequence, I can identify several key domain features:<br><br>- **GAF domain (cyclic nucleotide-binding domain)**: The region around &quot;FNCPENACRFLWQLRIRSSSVVDLFITSANWDNIAGISSILLSK&quot; matches the GAF domain structure, which senses small molecule ligands including cyclic nucleotides.<br><br>- **PDE (phosphodiesterase) catalytic domain**: The region containing conserved histidine residues (&quot;HAH&quot;, &quot;DH&quot; in &quot;ITAHQDHMNG&quot;) and the surrounding architecture is consistent with a PDE domain that hydrolyzes cGMP/cAMP.<br><br>- **P-loop NTPase/helicase domain**: The C-terminal region contains &quot;IDVGEGT&quot; (Walker A motif variant) and &quot;HIILTH&quot; (Walker B motif variant), characteristic of P-loop NTPases involved in protein remodeling or nucleic acid processing.<br><br>This domain combination (GAF + PDE + NTPase) is characteristic of proteins involved in intracellular metabolic sensing and stress response signaling. Specifically:<br><br>- **Heat stress (A)**: No HSP-like motifs, no DnaJ/HSP70 signature domains.<br>- **Nutrient deprivation (B)**: While possible, there&#x27;s no clear nutrient-sensing kinase domain (like AMPK/PI3K motifs).<br>- **DNA damage (C)**: No RING finger, UBA, or canonical DNA repair domains are present.<br>- **Mitochondrial stress (D)**: The GAF domain senses metabolic intermediates; the PDE domain regulates cyclic nucleotide pools (key mediators of mitochondrial unfolded protein response/UPRmt); the P-loop NTPase could participate in mitochondrial RNA/protein remodeling. This architecture is consistent with proteins induced during mitochondrial stress, where cells activate signaling cascades involving cyclic nucleotide-mediated pathways and protein quality control.<br><br>2. answer: D</td>

Actually, I recall now that in many serine hydrolases, the motif is better described as having the serine at a nucleophilic elbow, and the consensus is often written as G-X-S-X-G where:
- The first G is at the end of a β-strand
- The S is at the turn (the nucleophilic serine)
- The second G is at the beginning of the next β-strand or the start of an α-helix

So the pattern is: [β-strand]-G-X-S-X-G-[α-helix]
